\|\Delta w^{(-v)}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\ \lesssim\ B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

Combining gives

|Z_{v}-Z_{v,(v)}|\ \leq\ c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa\,B_{\phi}\,\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

For cross terms, by (
96
),

|Z_{u}-Z_{u,(v)}|\leq c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}\qquad(u\neq v).

Therefore,

\Big|\sum_{u\neq v}(Z_{u}-Z_{u,(v)})\Big|\leq(N-1)\cdot c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

Putting together and dividing by
N
yields an almost sure increment bound

\big|\tilde{I}_{2}^{(U)}-\tilde{I}_{2,(v)}^{(U)}\big|\leq b:=c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,B_{\phi}\,\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\cdot\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}+c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

(105)

Apply (
91
) with variance proxy (
104
)
and increment bound (
105
). Choose
\delta=1/(2N^{\prime})
so that
\log(2/\delta)\asymp\log N^{\prime}
.
On
\mathscr{E}
, this gives with probability at least
1-1/(2N^{\prime})-1/(2N^{\prime})

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\tilde{I}_{2}^{(U)}(x_{0};\theta)|&\leq\sqrt{2V\log(2/\delta)}+\frac{b\log(2/\delta)}{3}\\
&\leq c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{NN^{\prime}}}\cdot\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}+\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}\Big)+c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}\\
&\leq c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\cdot\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{N},\end{split}

(106)

where we used
N^{\prime}\geq N
to absorb the
N^{\prime}
factors into
N
, and absorbed lower-order terms into constants.

Combining (
106
) with the GAP bound (
90
) yields

|I_{2}^{(U)}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\cdot\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{N}+c\,B_{f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

(107)

Part B: labeled interaction
I_{2}^{(R)}
.

By reproducing from (
87
),

I_{2}^{(R)}(x_{0};\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta w(X_{i})\,r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[\Delta w(X)\,r(Y,X;\theta)].

Define LOO weights on the pooled design exactly as in Lemma
6
:
for each labeled index
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, remove the rank-one term
(1/N^{\prime})K_{X_{i}}\otimes K_{X_{i}}
from
\hat{T}_{K}

and write the resulting
\Delta w^{(-i)}:=\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{x_{0},\lambda}-w_{x_{0},\lambda}
.
Add and subtract the LOO quantities to decompose
I_{2}^{(R)}
into a LOO-centered main term plus a LOO GAP term.
The GAP term is controlled by the same arguments as Lemma
6

with
u
replaced by
r
and
B_{f}(\theta)
replaced by
B_{Y-f}(\theta)
, and we omit the repetition.

The remaining LOO-centered main term is a function of independent variables with bounded replace-one increments,
hence it obeys the same exponential Efron–Stein bound as in Part A above.
Using Lemma
5
, with probability at least
1-(N^{\prime})^{-1}
,

|I_{2}^{(R)}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq c\,B_{\phi}\,D(\lambda)\frac{B_{Y-f}(\theta)}{n}\,\log N^{\prime}+c\,B_{Y-f}(\theta)\,\kappa^{2}\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

(108)

On the intersection of the high-probability events used above: on the stability event
\mathscr{E}
,
(i) Lemma
5
and the uniform LOO version (
103
) (failure rate
(2N^{\prime})^{-1}
),
(ii) Lemma
6
,
we have from (
108
) (failure rate
(2N^{\prime})^{-1}
) and (
107
) (failure rate
(2N^{\prime})^{-1}
) that

\displaystyle|I_{2}(x_{0};\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq|I_{2}^{(R)}(x_{0};\theta)|+|I_{2}^{(U)}(x_{0};\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\,B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\Big(\frac{B_{Y-f}(\theta)}{n}+\frac{B_{f}(\theta)}{N}\Big)\log N^{\prime}+c_{2}\,(B_{f}(\theta)+B_{Y-f}(\theta))\kappa^{2}B_{\phi}^{2}\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

This is exactly (
35
), and a union bound
and adjustment of absolute constants yield overall success probability at least
1-3(2N^{\prime})^{-1}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8
.

Step 1: whitening identity.

Recall

A(\lambda):=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\,(\hat{T}_{K}-T_{K})\,(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}.

Then

\hat{T}_{K}+\lambda I=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{1/2},

and hence

(\hat{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)^{-1}(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}.

(109)

Let

u:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x_{0}},\qquad v:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\mu(\theta).

Note that

\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\big\langle K_{x_{0}},(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=D(x_{0};\lambda).

Combining
\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}=(\hat{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}
with (
109
) gives the exact identity

\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)^{-1}u,\qquad w_{x_{0},\lambda}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}u,

hence

\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}-w_{x_{0},\lambda}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\Big(\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)^{-1}-I\Big)u=-(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)^{-1}A(\lambda)u,

and therefore

I_{3}(x_{0};\theta)=-\big\langle\big(I+A(\lambda)\big)^{-1}A(\lambda)u,\ v\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

(110)

Step 2: stability event and decomposition.

Recall that
\mathscr{E}:=\{\|A(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
, on
\mathscr{E}
,
\|(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2
and we can expand

(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)=A(\lambda)-(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)^{2}.

Plugging into (
110
) yields the exact decomposition on
\mathscr{E}
:

I_{3}(x_{0};\theta)=-\underbrace{\langle A(\lambda)u,\ v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}_{=:T_{1}}+\underbrace{\Big\langle(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}_{=:T_{2}}.

Consequently, on
\mathscr{E}
,

|I_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq|T_{1}|+|T_{2}|\leq|T_{1}|+2\,\big|\langle A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|=|T_{1}|+2\,\big|\langle A(\lambda)u,\ A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|.

(111)

Step 3: reduce
T_{1}=\langle A(\lambda)u,v\rangle
to empirical mean.

Let
g_{x}:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x}\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

A(\lambda)=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\big(g_{\bar{X}_{i}}\otimes g_{\bar{X}_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[g_{X}\otimes g_{X}]\big),

and therefore

\langle A(\lambda)u,\ v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(\langle g_{\bar{X}_{i}},u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\langle g_{\bar{X}_{i}},v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbb{E}[\langle g_{X},u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\langle g_{X},v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}]\Big).

Define the deterministic functions

\displaystyle a(x)

\displaystyle:=\langle g_{x},u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle K_{x},(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=w_{x_{0},\lambda}(x),

(112)

\displaystyle b(x)

\displaystyle:=\langle g_{x},v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle K_{x},(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

(113)

Then

T_{1}=\langle A(\lambda)u,\ v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\Big),\qquad Z_{i}:=a(\bar{X}_{i})b(\bar{X}_{i}),

(114)

and
\{Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}
are i.i.d.

Step 4: bound
b(\cdot)
.

By
\mu(\theta)=T_{K}\eta(\cdot;\theta)
,

b(\cdot)=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\mu(\theta)=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}T_{K}\eta(\cdot;\theta).

In the eigenbasis of
T_{K}
,
(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}T_{K}
has eigenvalues
\mu_{j}/(\mu_{j}+\lambda)\in[0,1]
,
so
\|(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}T_{K}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
. Hence

\|b\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}},\qquad\|b\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(115)

Step 5: variance proxy.

First,

\mathrm{Var}(Z)\leq\mathbb{E}[Z^{2}]\leq\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[a(X)^{2}].

Since
a(X)=w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[a(X)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},T_{K}w_{x_{0},\lambda}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},(T_{K}+\lambda I)w_{x_{0},\lambda}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=w_{x_{0},\lambda}(x_{0})=D(x_{0};\lambda).

Combining with (
115
) gives

\mathrm{Var}(Z)\leq\kappa^{2}\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,D(x_{0};\lambda).

(116)

Step 6: envelope bound.

By Cauchy–Schwarz in
\mathcal{H}
,

|a(x)|=|\langle g_{x},u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq\|g_{x}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\sqrt{D(x;\lambda)}\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

(117)

Under Assumption
4
, we have for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
,

D(x;\lambda)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\phi_{j}(x)^{2}\leq B_{\phi}^{2}\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}=B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda),

and hence (
117
) implies

\|a\|_{\infty}\leq B_{\phi}\,\sqrt{D(\lambda)}\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

(118)

Therefore, using
\|Z-\mathbb{E}Z\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|Z\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}
and (
115
),

\|Z-\mathbb{E}Z\|_{\infty}\leq 2\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,B_{\phi}\,\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

(119)

Step 7: scalar Bernstein for
T_{1}=\langle A(\lambda)u,v\rangle
.

Apply Bernstein’s inequality to the i.i.d. mean in (
114
).
For any
t\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

|T_{1}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\,\mathrm{Var}(Z)\,t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{2\,\|Z-\mathbb{E}Z\|_{\infty}\,t}{3N^{\prime}}.

Plugging (
116
) and (
119
) yields

\displaystyle|T_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{4}{3}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\,\frac{t}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\left(1+\frac{4\sqrt{2}B_{\phi}}{3}\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\right).

(120)

Step 8: the remainder term
T_{2}
.

We bound
T_{2}
using Cauchy–Schwarz in
\mathcal{H}
:

\big|\langle A(\lambda)u,\ A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|\leq\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\|A(\lambda)v\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(121)

Next, note that
A(\lambda)u
and
A(\lambda)v
are themselves empirical means of centered
\mathcal{H}
-valued random variables.
Indeed, using
g_{x}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x}
and (
112
)–(
113
),

\displaystyle A(\lambda)u

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(a(\bar{X}_{i})\,g_{\bar{X}_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[a(X)g_{X}]\Big),

(122)

\displaystyle A(\lambda)v

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(b(\bar{X}_{i})\,g_{\bar{X}_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[b(X)g_{X}]\Big).

(123)

Applying a Hilbert-valued Bernstein inequality to (
122
)–(
123
) with the same parameter
t\geq 1

and using (
118
), (
115
), and
\|g_{x}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=D(x;\lambda)\leq B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda)
under Assumption
4
,
one obtains: with probability at least
1-4e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{u}\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right),

(124)

\displaystyle\|A(\lambda)v\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right),

(125)

for absolute constants
C_{u},C_{v}
depending only on
(B_{\phi},\kappa)
.
Combining (
121
)–(
125
) yields that, with probability at least
1-4e^{-t}
,

\big|\langle A(\lambda)u,\ A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|\leq C_{uv}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right)^{2},

(126)

where
C_{uv}:=C_{u}C_{v}
.
Set
\rho_{N^{\prime}}(t)\ :=\ D(\lambda)\sqrt{\frac{t}{N^{\prime}}}
.
Under
D(\lambda)\sqrt{\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}}\to 0
and
t\asymp\log N^{\prime}
, we have
\rho_{N^{\prime}}(t)\to 0
.
In particular
\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\to 0
.
Then

\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right)^{2}=\left(s+s^{2}\right)^{2}\leq 4s^{2}=4\,\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\qquad\text{whenever }s:=\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\to 0,

so (
126
) implies the quadratic contribution satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}2\,\big|\langle A(\lambda)u,\ A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|&\leq 8C_{uv}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\,\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\\
&=8C_{uv}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\,\rho_{N^{\prime}}(t).\end{split}

(127)

Thus, once
N^{\prime}
is large enough so that
\rho_{N^{\prime}}(t)\to 0
, the remainder term in (
111
) is controlled by the same

\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}
scale as
T_{1}
, and can be absorbed into the overall constant
multiplying the leading rate.

Step 9.

Choose
t:=\log(4N^{\prime})
, so that
2e^{-t}\leq(N^{\prime})^{-1}
and
4e^{-t}\leq(N^{\prime})^{-1}
.
Then
the scalar Bernstein event in Step 7 holds with failure probability at most
(N^{\prime})^{-1}
,
and the Hilbert-valued Bernstein event in Step 8 holds with failure probability at most
(N^{\prime})^{-1}
.
By a union bound, their intersection holds with probability at least
1-2(N^{\prime})^{-1}
on event
\mathscr{E}
.

On this intersection, first use (
120
) to absorb the linear Bernstein term:
for
N^{\prime}
large enough so that
\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\to c_{0}
and
\frac{4\sqrt{2}B_{\phi}}{3}c_{0}\leq 1
,

|T_{1}|\leq 2\sqrt{2}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}.

(128)

Next, (
127
) and the condition
8C_{uv}\rho_{N^{\prime}}(t)\leq\sqrt{2}
yield
2\big|\langle A(\lambda)u,\ A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big|\leq|T_{1}|
.
Plugging these into (
111
) gives, for all
N^{\prime}\geq N^{\prime}_{0}
,

\displaystyle|I_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq|T_{1}|+2|\langle A(\lambda)u,A(\lambda)v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq 4\sqrt{2}\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}},

Using
t=\log(4N^{\prime})\leq\log N^{\prime}+\log 4
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq 8\,\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}},

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

First, we introduce the derivative analogues of
\hat{\mu}(\theta)
and
\mu(\theta)
:

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}^{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\theta}r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)\,K_{X_{i}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u=1}^{N}\partial_{\theta}u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)\,K_{\tilde{X}_{u}}\ \in\ \mathcal{H},

\displaystyle\mu^{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)\,K_{X}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)\,K_{X}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\partial_{\theta}\ell(Y;\theta)\,K_{X}\big]\ \in\ \mathcal{H}.

By the reproducing property,

J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\mu^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},\qquad\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\langle\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda},\hat{\mu}^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

(129)

Step 1: exact decomposition.

From (
129
),

\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\langle\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda},\hat{\mu}^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\mu^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

For any
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
, define

\displaystyle J_{1}(x_{0};\theta):

\displaystyle=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\hat{\mu}^{1}(\theta)-\mu^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},

\displaystyle\ J_{2}(x_{0};\theta):

\displaystyle=\langle\Delta w,\hat{\mu}^{1}(\theta)-\mu^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},

\displaystyle J_{3}(x_{0};\theta):

\displaystyle=\langle\Delta w,\mu^{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Then the exact decomposition holds:

\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=J_{1}(x_{0};\theta)+J_{2}(x_{0};\theta)+J_{3}(x_{0};\theta).

(130)

Step 2:
J_{1}
follows
I_{1}
.

Write
J_{1}
as the sum of two independent centered empirical means:

\displaystyle J_{1}(x_{0};\theta)=

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X_{i})\partial_{\theta}r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)]\Big\}

\displaystyle+\Big\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u=1}^{N}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(\tilde{X}_{u})\partial_{\theta}u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)]\Big\}.

Under Assumption
2
, for
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
,

|\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)|\leq G_{Y-f}(\theta),\qquad|\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)|\leq G_{f}(\theta).

As in Lemma
4
,
\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]\leq D(x_{0};\lambda)
.
Applying the same scalar Bernstein argument as in Lemma
4

with envelopes
G_{Y-f}(\theta),G_{f}(\theta)
and the same choice
t\asymp\log N^{\prime}

yields: with probability at least

1-8(N^{\prime})^{-1}
,

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}|J_{1}(x_{0};\theta)|\ \leq\ 2\sqrt{2\log N^{\prime}}\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\frac{G_{Y-f}(\theta)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{G_{f}(\theta)}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

(131)

Step 3:
J_{2}
follows
I_{2}
on
\mathscr{E}
.

On the stability event
\mathscr{E}
from Lemma
3
,
the proof of Lemma
7
applies verbatim after replacing

B_{Y-f}(\theta),B_{f}(\theta)
by the uniform derivative envelopes

G_{Y-f}(\theta),G_{f}(\theta)
. In particular, there exists an absolute constant
c_{2}>0
such that,
on
\mathscr{E}
, with probability at least
1-2(N^{\prime})^{-1}
,

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}|J_{2}(x_{0};\theta)|\ \lesssim\ B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\Big(\frac{G_{Y-f}(\theta)}{n}+\frac{G_{f}(\theta)}{N}\Big)\log N^{\prime}+(G_{f}+G_{Y-f})\kappa^{2}B_{\phi}^{2}\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}.

(132)

Step 4:
J_{3}
on
\mathscr{E}
.

We now rework the
I_{3}
-type argument for
J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)
.
The key difference from Lemma
8
is that
\mu^{1}(\theta)
cannot be
written as
T_{K}\eta(\cdot;\theta)
for some
\eta(\cdot;\theta)\in\mathcal{H}
; instead we can write

\mu^{1}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta}(X)K_{X}]
for a function
g_{\theta}\in L^{2}(\rho_{X})
, which
introduces an extra
\sqrt{D(\lambda)}
factor in the envelope.
Recall

A(\lambda):=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\,(\hat{T}_{K}-T_{K})\,(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2},\qquad u:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x_{0}}.

On
\mathscr{E}=\{\|A(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
, we have
\|(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2
and

\hat{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}-w_{x_{0},\lambda}=-(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)u.

Let

v_{1}(\theta):=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\mu^{1}(\theta).

Then the exact identity holds:

J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)=-\big\langle(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Expanding as in Lemma
8
, on
\mathscr{E}
we can write
(I+A)^{-1}A=A-(I+A)^{-1}A^{2}
,
so

J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)=-\underbrace{\langle A(\lambda)u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}_{=:T_{1,J}(\theta)}+\underbrace{\langle(I+A(\lambda))^{-1}A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}_{=:T_{2,J}(\theta)}.

Hence on
\mathscr{E}
,

|J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq|T_{1,J}(\theta)|+|T_{2,J}(\theta)|\leq|T_{1,J}(\theta)|+2\,|\langle A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|.

(133)

Let
g_{x}:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x}\in\mathcal{H}
so that

A(\lambda)=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(g_{\bar{X}_{j}}\otimes g_{\bar{X}_{j}}-\mathbb{E}[g_{X}\otimes g_{X}]\Big).

Exactly as in the
I_{3}
proof, define

a(x):=\langle g_{x},u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=w_{x_{0},\lambda}(x),\qquad b_{1}(x;\theta):=\langle g_{x},v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\big\langle K_{x},(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\mu^{1}(\theta)\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Then

T_{1,J}(\theta)=\langle A(\lambda)u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{j}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}[Z(\theta)]\Big),\qquad Z_{j}(\theta):=a(\bar{X}_{j})\,b_{1}(\bar{X}_{j};\theta),

(134)

and
\{Z_{j}(\theta)\}_{j=1}^{N^{\prime}}
are i.i.d. for each fixed
\theta
.
Define the conditional derivative function

g_{\theta}(x):=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\theta}\ell(Y;\theta)|X=x]\in L^{2}(\rho_{X}).

Under Assumption
2
,
|g_{\theta}(x)|\leq G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta)
for
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
, hence

\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\leq G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta)\qquad\text{uniformly over }\theta\in\Theta_{0}.

Using the Mercer expansion with
(\mu_{j},\phi_{j})
and the standard identity

\mu^{1}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta}(X)K_{X}]
, one obtains

b_{1}(x;\theta)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\,\langle g_{\theta},\phi_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\,\phi_{j}(x).

Therefore, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|b_{1}(x;\theta)|&\leq\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\,\Big(\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\,\phi_{j}(x)^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&=\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\sqrt{D(x;\lambda)}\leq B_{\phi}\,\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\sqrt{D(\lambda)}.\end{split}

(135)

We will also use the companion bound:
in the RKHS eigenbasis
\{\psi_{j}\}
with
\psi_{j}:=\sqrt{\mu_{j}}\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
,

\mu^{1}(\theta)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\sqrt{\mu_{j}}\,\langle g_{\theta},\phi_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}}\,\psi_{j},\quad\to\quad\|v_{1}(\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\,\langle g_{\theta},\phi_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2},

so for
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
,

\|v_{1}(\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\ \leq\ G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta)<\infty.

(136)

Using (
135
),
\mathrm{Var}(Z(\theta))\leq\mathbb{E}[Z(\theta)^{2}]\leq\|b_{1}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[a(X)^{2}]
.
As in Lemma
8
,
\mathbb{E}[a(X)^{2}]\leq D(x_{0};\lambda)
.
Hence, uniformly over
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
,

\mathrm{Var}(Z(\theta))\ \leq\ B_{\phi}^{2}\,\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}\,D(\lambda)\,D(x_{0};\lambda).

(137)

For the envelope, use
\|Z-\mathbb{E}Z\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|Z\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}\|b_{1}\|_{\infty}
.
As in Lemma
8
,

\|a\|_{\infty}=\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)},

and combining with (
135
) yields, uniformly over
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
,

\|Z(\theta)-\mathbb{E}Z(\theta)\|_{\infty}\ \leq\ C\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\,D(\lambda)\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

(138)

Apply Bernstein to (
134
) with
t=\log(4N^{\prime})
.
With probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
, for each fixed
\theta
,

|T_{1,J}(\theta)|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\,\mathrm{Var}(Z(\theta))\,t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{2\,\|Z(\theta)-\mathbb{E}Z(\theta)\|_{\infty}\,t}{3N^{\prime}}.

Using (
137
)–(
138
) and
\|g_{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}\leq G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta)
gives,
after absorbing constants,

\displaystyle|T_{1,J}(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq C\,B_{\phi}\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\,t}{N^{\prime}}}\Bigg(1+C^{\prime}\,B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}\Bigg).

(139)

Under (
36
), with
t\asymp\log N^{\prime}
, we have

\sqrt{D(\lambda)t/N^{\prime}}\to 0
, so the parenthetical factor in (
139
)
is bounded by (say)
2
for large
N^{\prime}
, yielding

|T_{1,J}(\theta)|\ \leq\ C\,B_{\phi}\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\,\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}}.

(140)

From (
133
) and Cauchy–Schwarz,

|\langle A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq\|A(\lambda)^{2}u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\|v_{1}(\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\|A(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\|v_{1}(\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

On
\mathscr{E}
,
\|A(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2
, hence

|T_{2,J}(\theta)|\leq 2\,|\langle A(\lambda)^{2}u,\ v_{1}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\|v_{1}(\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(141)

It remains to bound
\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}
. As in Step 8 of the
I_{3}
proof,

A(\lambda)u
is a centered empirical mean of
\mathcal{H}
-valued random variables:

A(\lambda)u=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\prime}}\Big(a(\bar{X}_{j})\,g_{\bar{X}_{j}}-\mathbb{E}[a(X)g_{X}]\Big),\qquad g_{x}:=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}K_{x}.

Applying a Hilbert-valued Bernstein inequality
and using
\|g_{x}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=D(x;\lambda)\leq B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda)
together with

\|a\|_{\infty}\leq B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}
,
we obtain: for
t=\log(4N^{\prime})
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

\|A(\lambda)u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C_{u}\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right),

(142)

for an absolute constant
C_{u}
depending only on
(B_{\phi},\kappa)
.
Combining (
141
), (
142
), and (
136
) yields

\displaystyle|T_{2,J}(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{u}\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\,\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}}+\frac{D(\lambda)t}{N^{\prime}}\right).

(143)

Under (
36
) (with
t\asymp\log N^{\prime}
), the second term is
o(1)
and is dominated by
the leading
\sqrt{D(\lambda)t/N^{\prime}}
term. In particular, for all large
N^{\prime}
,

|T_{2,J}(\theta)|\ \leq\ C\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}}.

(144)

Combine (
133
), (
140
), and (
144
) (with
t=\log(4N^{\prime})
).
On
\mathscr{E}
, with failure probability
O((N^{\prime})^{-1})
, we obtain

|J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\ \leq\ C\,B_{\phi}\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}}.

(145)

Finally, note that if one wishes to express this in a worst-case leverage form, then using

\sup_{x}D(x;\lambda)\leq B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda)
and
D(x_{0};\lambda)\leq B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda)
gives

|J_{3}(x_{0};\theta)|\ \leq\ C\,B_{\phi}^{2}\,(G_{Y-f}(\theta)+G_{f}(\theta))\,D(\lambda)\sqrt{\frac{\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}},

which tends to
0
under (
36
).

Step 5: probability bookkeeping and uniform event.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{J1}
be the event on which (
131
) holds,
\mathscr{E}_{J2}
the event on which
(
132
) holds, and
\mathscr{E}_{J3}
the event on which (
145
) holds (all uniformly over
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
).
By Steps 2–4, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{J1}^{c})\leq 8(N^{\prime})^{-1},\qquad\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{J2}^{c}|\mathscr{E})\leq 3(2N^{\prime})^{-1},\qquad\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{J3}^{c}|\mathscr{E})\leq 2(N^{\prime})^{-1}.

Therefore, by a union bound,

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathscr{E}_{J1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J2}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J3}\big)\ \geq\ 1-11.5(N^{\prime})^{-1}.

(146)

Step 6: uniform control of
\hat{J}_{\lambda}-J_{\lambda}
.

On the intersection event in (
146
), combine
(
130
) with (
131
), (
132
), and (
145
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)+B_{\phi}D(\lambda)\Big(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{N}\Big)\log N^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2}B_{\phi}^{2}\frac{D(\lambda)}{N^{\prime}}+B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N^{\prime}}{N^{\prime}}}.

By the scaling conditions in (
36
), each term on the right-hand side
tends to
0
as
N^{\prime}\to\infty
(using also
n\wedge N\to\infty
and
N^{\prime}\geq n\wedge N
).
Hence there exists
N^{\prime}_{0}
such that for all
N^{\prime}\geq N^{\prime}_{0}
,

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|\ \leq\ c_{J}/2\qquad\text{on }\ \mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J2}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J3}.

Therefore, on the same event and for all
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
,

|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)|\geq|J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)|-\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|\geq c_{J}-c_{J}/2=c_{J}/2,

where we used Assumption
3
. This proves (
37
).
Finally,
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)^{-1}|\leq\frac{2}{c_{J}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Step 1: reduce to labeled variance.

By independence of the labeled and unlabeled samples,

V(x_{0};\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}(\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sigma^{2}_{f}(\theta)\ \geq\ \frac{1}{n}\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}(\theta).

Step 2.

By the law of total variance,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\,r\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\,\mathrm{Var}(r|X)\Big]+\mathrm{Var}\Big(w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\,\mathbb{E}[r|X]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\,\mathrm{Var}(r|X)\Big]\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\big],

where the last inequality uses Assumption
5
.

Step 3: spectral expansion.

Using the Mercer expansion
K(x,x^{\prime})=\sum_{j\geq 1}\mu_{j}\phi_{j}(x)\phi_{j}(x^{\prime})
,
the localization weight admits

w_{x_{0},\lambda}(x)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\,\phi_{j}(x_{0})\phi_{j}(x),

hence, by orthonormality of
\{\phi_{j}\}
in
L^{2}(\rho_{X})
,

\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\big]=\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\Big)^{2}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}=\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\mu_{j}^{2}}{(\mu_{j}+\lambda)^{2}}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}.

(147)

Step 4: head lower bound and the factor
c(\lambda)
.

Restrict (
147
) to the head set
\{j:\mu_{j}\geq\lambda\}
.
For
\mu_{j}\geq\lambda
, one has
\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, and therefore

\Big(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\Big)^{2}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\big]\geq\sum_{j:\,\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\Big(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\Big)^{2}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j:\,\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}=\frac{c(\lambda)}{2}\,D(x_{0};\lambda),

where
c(\lambda):=\frac{\sum_{j:\,\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\,\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}}{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\ \in\ (0,1]
.
Putting this into Step 2 gives

\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}(\theta)\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]\ \geq\ \Big(\frac{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\,c(\lambda)}{2}\Big)\,D(x_{0};\lambda).

Step 5: uniform lower bound
c(\lambda)\geq c_{0}
.

By the proof of Lemma
2
, for some
C_{0}

\sum_{\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}\ \geq\ C_{0}^{2}\,\lambda^{-d/(2m)}.

Since
\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}
on
\{\mu_{j}\geq\lambda\}
, we obtain

\sum_{\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{j}+\lambda}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mu_{j}\geq\lambda}\phi_{j}(x_{0})^{2}\ \geq\ \frac{C_{0}^{2}}{2}\,\lambda^{-d/(2m)}.

(148)

By Proposition
1
,
D(x_{0};\lambda)\asymp\lambda^{-d/(2m)}
.
Therefore, by (
148
),
for all
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}]
,
c(\lambda)\geq\ c_{0}>0
,
which proves (
48
) and completes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Theoretical Results in Sections
4
,
5
, and
6

This appendix provides the complete theoretical proofs for the results presented in Sections
4
,
5
, and
6
under the two-fold sample-splitting setting, along with the proofs of all supporting technical lemmas. While the overarching proof strategy for the error bound (Theorem
1
) and asymptotic normality (Theorem
3
) closely follows the non-split framework established in Appendix
D.3
, the technical details are carefully adapted here to accommodate the cross-fitting structure. Furthermore, we include the proofs for Propositions
1
and
2
and the minimax lower bound (Theorem
2
). The remainder of this appendix is organized as follows: we first proceed to prove the main theorems and proposition in Appendix
E.1
, and detail the proofs of the required technical lemmas in Appendix
E.2
. Finally, Appendix
E.3
provides a detailed discussion on our technical novelties and a comprehensive comparison with classical KRR theory.

E.1
Proofs of Theorems in Sections
4
,
5
, and
6

We begin by introducing the notation that differs from that used in Appendix
D.3
.

Two-fold splitting on unlabeled covariates.

Randomly partition
\{1,\dots,N\}
into two disjoint folds
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}

(with
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=|\mathcal{I}_{2}|=N/2
for simplicity).
For each
m\in\{1,2\}
, define the fold-specific empirical covariance operator

\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}K_{\tilde{X}_{u}}\otimes K_{\tilde{X}_{u}},

and the corresponding empirical localization weight

\hat{w}^{(m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}:=\big(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I\big)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}\in\mathcal{H},\qquad\Delta w^{(m)}:=\hat{w}^{(m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}-w_{x_{0},\lambda}.

For labeled data, we apply the averaged weight

\overline{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}:=\frac{1}{2}\big(\hat{w}^{(1)}_{x_{0},\lambda}+\hat{w}^{(2)}_{x_{0},\lambda}\big),\qquad\Delta\overline{w}:=\overline{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}-w_{x_{0},\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}\big(\Delta w^{(1)}+\Delta w^{(2)}\big).

For unlabeled data, we evaluate out-of-fold: for
u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}
, we weight
\tilde{X}_{u}
using
\hat{w}^{(3-m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}
.

Localized PPI moments.

Recall that
\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\ell(Y;\theta)]
.
Define its two-fold cross-fitted empirical estimator is

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}(X_{i})\,r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\hat{w}^{(3-m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}(\tilde{X}_{u})\,u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta).

(149)

We also define the oracle version that uses the population weight
w_{x_{0},\lambda}
,

\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X_{i})\,r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u=1}^{N}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(\tilde{X}_{u})\,u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta).

The true target is defined by
\eta(x_{0};\theta_{0}(x_{0}))=0
, and the estimator
\hat{\theta}(x_{0})
is any solution to

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\hat{\theta}(x_{0}))=0
.

Decomposition.

The reproducing property gives

\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\hat{\mu}(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

and

\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Therefore,

\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\underbrace{\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},\hat{\mu}(\theta)-\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}_{=:I_{1}(x_{0};\theta)}.

Next, write the additional error induced by estimating the localization weight as

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta\overline{w}(X_{i})\,r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\Delta w^{(3-m)}(\tilde{X}_{u})\,u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta).

We split this term into a mean component and a centered fluctuation component.
Define

I^{\mathrm{split}}_{3}(x_{0};\theta):=\langle\Delta\overline{w},\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},

and

I^{\mathrm{split}}_{2}(x_{0};\theta):=\big(\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big)-I^{\mathrm{split}}_{3}(x_{0};\theta).

Combining the above identities yields the three-term decomposition

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=I_{1}(x_{0};\theta)+I^{\mathrm{split}}_{2}(x_{0};\theta)+I^{\mathrm{split}}_{3}(x_{0};\theta).

Jacobians.

Recall that
J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}\ell(Y;\theta)]

and define the empirical Jacobian corresponding to (
149
),

\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta):=\partial_{\theta}\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{w}_{x_{0},\lambda}(X_{i})\,\partial_{\theta}r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\hat{w}^{(3-m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}(\tilde{X}_{u})\,\partial_{\theta}u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta).

Operator stability with two-fold splitting.

We next present a technical lemma establishing operator stability under the two-fold sample-splitting regime, which is instrumental for our subsequent theoretical analysis. This result can be viewed as the cross-fitted counterpart to Lemma
3
. Since its proof follows identical arguments to those used for the non-split version, it is omitted here.

Lemma 11
.

Write
\tau:=\frac{d}{2m}\in(0,1)
.
For each fold
m\in\{1,2\}
, define

A^{(m)}(\lambda):=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}-T_{K}\big)(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}.

Then there exists a constant
C_{a}:=C_{1}^{2}\,\tau^{\tau}(1-\tau)^{1-\tau}
such that,
for every
\delta\in(0,1]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{4\,C_{a}}{3\,|\mathcal{I}_{m}|\,\lambda^{\tau}}\,\log\Big(\frac{4\,\lambda^{\tau}D(\lambda)}{\delta}\Big)+\sqrt{\frac{2\,C_{a}}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|\,\lambda^{\tau}}\,\log\Big(\frac{4\,\lambda^{\tau}D(\lambda)}{\delta}\Big)}.

Moreover, under Proposition
1
,
D(\lambda)\asymp\lambda^{-\tau}
,
taking
\delta=|\mathcal{I}_{m}|^{-1}
yields

\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{8\,C_{a}}{3\,|\mathcal{I}_{m}|\,\lambda^{\tau}}\,\log|\mathcal{I}_{m}|+\sqrt{\frac{4\,C_{a}}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|\,\lambda^{\tau}}\,\log|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}.

Let the fold-wise stability event be
\mathscr{E}_{m}:=\{\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
.
If the regularization satisfies

\lambda\ \geq\ \Big(\frac{256\,C_{a}\,\log|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}\Big)^{1/\tau}=\Big(\frac{256\,C_{a}\,\log|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}\Big)^{2m/d},

then
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{m})\geq 1-|\mathcal{I}_{m}|^{-1}
.
In particular, under two-fold equal splitting
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=|\mathcal{I}_{2}|=N/2
, defining
\mathscr{E}:=\mathscr{E}_{1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{2}
,
a union bound gives
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})\geq 1-4/N
.

Proof of Proposition
1
.
First, we provide the proof of Proposition
1
in Section
4
.

Proof of Proposition
1
.

First, for Sobolev space
H^{m}
, the eigenvalues of
T_{K}
satisfy
\mu_{j}(T_{K})\asymp j^{-2m/d}

Fischer and Steinwart 2020
, then direct calculation leads to
D(\lambda)\ \asymp\ \lambda^{-d/(2m)}
. Next, we focus on
D(x_{0};\lambda)
.
For
h,g\in\mathcal{H}
, by the definition of a rank-one operator and the reproducing property

\langle h,K_{x}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=h(x)
,

\big\langle h,(K_{X}\otimes K_{X})g\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle h,K_{X}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\langle K_{X},g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=h(X)\,g(X).

Taking expectation yields
\langle h,T_{K}g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathbb{E}[h(X)g(X)]
.
In particular,

\langle h,T_{K}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathbb{E}[h(X)^{2}]=\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}.

(150)

Step 1: variational representation of
D(x_{0};\lambda)
.

Let
T_{\lambda}:=T_{K}+\lambda I
on
\mathcal{H}
, which is self-adjoint and strictly positive.
For any fixed
g\in\mathcal{H}
,

\langle g,T_{\lambda}^{-1}g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H},\ h\neq 0}\frac{\langle h,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{\langle h,T_{\lambda}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}=\sup_{\langle h,T_{\lambda}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}\langle h,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}.

(151)

Apply (
151
) with
g=K_{x_{0}}
.
Since
\langle h,K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=h(x_{0})
and by (
150
),

\langle h,T_{\lambda}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle h,T_{K}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}.

Therefore

D(x_{0};\lambda)=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H},\ h\neq 0}\frac{h(x_{0})^{2}}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}.

(152)

Step 2: lower bound
D(x_{0};\lambda)\gtrsim\lambda^{-d/(2m)}
.

Because the density of
\rho_{X}
is bounded above/below on
\mathcal{X}
,

\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}
is equivalent to the Lebesgue
L^{2}
norm on
\mathcal{X}
:

\rho_{0}^{1/2}\|h\|_{L^{2}(dx)}\leq\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\leq\rho_{1}^{1/2}\|h\|_{L^{2}(dx)}.

Hence, throughout the proof we may invoke Sobolev inequalities stated with
L^{2}(dx)

and replace them by
L^{2}(\rho_{X})
at the cost of constants depending only on
\rho_{0},\rho_{1}
.
We keep the notation
\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}
for consistency.

Let
R:=\lambda^{-1/2}
and consider the subset

\mathscr{C}_{R}:=\{h\in\mathcal{H}:\ \|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq R\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\}
.
For any
h\in\mathscr{C}_{R}
,

\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\leq\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda R^{2}\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}=2\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}.

Plugging this restriction into (
152
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}D(x_{0};\lambda)&\geq\sup_{h\in\mathscr{C}_{R},\ h\neq 0}\frac{h(x_{0})^{2}}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{2}\sup_{h\in\mathscr{C}_{R},\ h\neq 0}\frac{h(x_{0})^{2}}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg(\sup_{h\in\mathscr{C}_{R},\ h\neq 0}\frac{|h(x_{0})|}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}}\Bigg)^{2}.\end{split}

(153)

Now invoke Lemma
1
and choose
\lambda_{0}\in(0,1)
so that
R=\lambda^{-1/2}\geq R_{0}
whenever
\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}
.
Combining with (
153
) gives, for
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}]
,

D(x_{0};\lambda)\ \geq\ \frac{1}{2}C_{0}^{2}\,R^{d/m}\asymp\lambda^{-d/(2m)}.

Step 3: upper bound
D(x_{0};\lambda)\lesssim\lambda^{-d/(2m)}
.

Fix
h\in\mathcal{H}
,
h\neq 0
. Set
t:=\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}/\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\in(0,\infty)
.
By the second inequality in Lemma
1
,

|h(x_{0})|\leq\|h\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\,\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{1-\frac{d}{2m}}\,\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{d}{2m}}=C_{1}\,\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\,t^{\frac{d}{2m}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{h(x_{0})^{2}}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}\,t^{d/m}}{\|h\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}(1+\lambda t^{2})}=C\,\frac{t^{d/m}}{1+\lambda t^{2}}.

(154)

Taking the supremum over
h\neq 0
in (
152
) yields

D(x_{0};\lambda)\ \leq\ C\sup_{t>0}\frac{t^{d/m}}{1+\lambda t^{2}}.

Let
s:=\sqrt{\lambda}\,t
. Then

\sup_{t>0}\frac{t^{d/m}}{1+\lambda t^{2}}=\lambda^{-d/(2m)}\sup_{s>0}\frac{s^{d/m}}{1+s^{2}}
.
Since
m>d/2
, we have
d/m\in(0,2)
and hence
\sup_{s>0}s^{d/m}/(1+s^{2})<\infty
.
Thus, for all
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}]
,

D(x_{0};\lambda)\ \lesssim\lambda^{-d/(2m)}
.

∎

Bounds for
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
and
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Since the term
I_{1}
remains identical to that in Appendix
D.3
, we now provide the error bounds for the remaining two components,
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
and
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
, in the following lemma.

Lemma 12

(Two-fold splitting bounds for
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
and
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
)
.

Fix
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
and
\theta\in\Theta
.
Let
\mathscr{E}_{m}:=\{\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
be as in Lemma
11
and set
\mathscr{E}:=\mathscr{E}_{1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{2}
.

(i)
Assume additionally
D(\lambda)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\to 0
.
Then for all sufficiently large
N
, with probability at least
1-\frac{12}{N}
,

|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq 8\sqrt{2}\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}.

(ii)
Let
c_{0}>0
be the absolute constant in Lemma
5
.
Then with probability at least
1-\frac{6}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}
,
we have

|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)|\leq B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left[(2c_{0}+4)B_{Y-f}(\theta)\frac{\log n}{n}+\Big(c_{0}B_{Y-f}(\theta)+(4c_{0}+8)B_{f}(\theta)\Big)\frac{\log N}{N}\right].

Jacobian stability and invertibility.

We analyze the stability and invertibility of the empirical Jacobian under two-fold splitting.

Lemma 13
.

Under Assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
, suppose that the regularization level satisfies

D(\lambda)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{n\wedge N}\to 0.

(155)

Let
\mathscr{E}_{m}:=\{\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
be as in
Lemma
11
and set
\mathscr{E}:=\mathscr{E}_{1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{2}
.
Set
G_{Y-f}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}G_{Y-f}(\theta)
and
G_{f}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}G_{f}(\theta)
,
where
G_{Y-f}(\theta),G_{f}(\theta)
are the derivative envelopes in Assumption
2
.
Then on the event
\mathscr{E}
, for all sufficiently large
N
, with probability at least
1-\frac{26}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}
,
we have

\inf_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|\geq c_{J}/2.

(156)

Consequently, on
\mathscr{E}
and the same event,
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)^{-1}\big|\leq\frac{2}{c_{J}}
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.
We provide the following proof.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Step 1: linear expansion around
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
.

By definition,

\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})=(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})).

Since
\hat{\theta}(x_{0})\in\Theta_{0}
solves
\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\hat{\theta}(x_{0}))=0
and
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})\in\Theta_{0}
solves
\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=0
, the mean-value theorem yields the existence of some
\tilde{\theta}
between
\hat{\theta}(x_{0})
and
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
such that

0=\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\hat{\theta}(x_{0}))=\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta})\big(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big),

hence

\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})=-\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta})^{-1}\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})).

(157)

Step 2: control
(\hat{J}_{\lambda})^{-1}
on
\Theta_{0}
.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{m}:=\{\|A^{(m)}(\lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/2\}
and
\mathscr{E}:=\mathscr{E}_{1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{2}
as in Lemma
11
. By Lemma
11
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{1}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{2}^{c})\leq\frac{1}{2N}+\frac{1}{2N}=\frac{1}{N}.

By Lemma
13
, on
\mathscr{E}
and for all sufficiently large
N
, with probability at least
1-\frac{26}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}
,

\inf_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|\geq c_{J}/2,\qquad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)^{-1}\big|\leq\frac{2}{c_{J}}.

Define

\mathscr{E}_{J}:=\Big\{\inf_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)\big|\geq c_{J}/2\Big\}.

Then

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J})\geq 1-\frac{1}{N}-\frac{26}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}=1-\frac{27}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}.

(158)

On
\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J}
, using (
157
),

|\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})|\leq\frac{2}{c_{J}}\big|\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\big|.

(159)

Step 3: control
\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))
by
I_{1}+I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Since
\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=0
, the split-version exact decomposition gives

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))-\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})).

Therefore,

\big|\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\big|\leq|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|.

(160)

Step 3a: high-probability events and probability bookkeeping.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{I_{1}}
be the event on which Lemma
4
yields its bound at
\theta=\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
,
let
\mathscr{E}_{I_{2}}
be the event on which Lemma
12
(ii) holds at
\theta=\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
,
and let
\mathscr{E}_{I_{3}}
be the event on which Lemma
12
(i) holds at
\theta=\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
.
Then for all sufficiently large
n,N
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{I_{1}}^{c})\leq\frac{8}{n+N},\qquad\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{I_{2}}^{c})\leq\frac{6}{N}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}+\frac{4}{N^{2}},\qquad\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{I_{3}}^{c})\leq\frac{12}{N}.

Define the global event

\mathscr{G}:=\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{E}_{J}\cap\mathscr{E}_{I_{1}}\cap\mathscr{E}_{I_{2}}\cap\mathscr{E}_{I_{3}}.

Using (
158
) and the union bound together with
\frac{8}{n+N}\leq\frac{8}{N}
, we get

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{G})\geq 1-\left(\frac{27}{N}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}+\frac{4}{N^{2}}\right)-\frac{8}{N}-\left(\frac{6}{N}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}+\frac{4}{N^{2}}\right)-\frac{12}{N}=1-\frac{53}{N}-\frac{4}{n^{2}}-\frac{8}{N^{2}}.

Step 3b: plug in
I_{1}
and
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

On
\mathscr{G}
, Lemma
4
gives

|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(n+N)}\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\frac{B_{Y-f}(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{B_{f}(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

(161)

Also, Lemma
12
(i) gives

|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|\leq 8\sqrt{2}\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}.

(162)

Step 3c: absorb
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Under Proposition
1
and the regime in (
155
), the bound in Lemma
12
(ii) implies that on
\mathscr{G}
and for all sufficiently large
n,N
,

|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|\leq|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|.

Therefore, on
\mathscr{G}
and for all sufficiently large
n,N
,

|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|\leq 2|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+2|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|.

Combining with (
159
) and (
160
) yields, on
\mathscr{G}
,

|\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})|\leq\frac{4}{c_{J}}\left(|I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|+|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|\right).

Plugging in (
161
) and (
162
) gives

\displaystyle|\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\sqrt{2}}{c_{J}}\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log(n+N)}\left(\frac{B_{Y-f}(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{B_{f}(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

(163)

\displaystyle+\frac{32\sqrt{2}}{c_{J}}\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}.

Step 4: bias bound for
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})
.

Because
\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=0
and
\theta_{0}(x_{0})\in\Theta_{0}
, a mean-value expansion yields the existence of
\bar{\theta}
between
\theta_{0}(x_{0})
and
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
such that

0=\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{0}(x_{0}))+J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\bar{\theta})\big(\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})\big).

By Assumption
3
,
|J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\bar{\theta})|\geq c_{J}
, hence

|\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})|\leq\frac{1}{c_{J}}|\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{0}(x_{0}))|.

By the regularization bias bound (
25
),

|\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})|\leq\frac{1}{c_{J}}\|\eta(\cdot;\theta_{0}(x_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\lambda D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

(164)

On
\mathscr{G}
, combine (
163
) and (
164
) to obtain (
11
). The probability statement is exactly
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{G})\geq 1-\frac{53}{N}-\frac{4}{n^{2}}-\frac{8}{N^{2}}
from Step 3(a). This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.
We proceed to prove the asymptotic result in Theorem
3
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

First, we prove

\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big)}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}\to N(0,1).

(165)

Step 1: local linearization at
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
.

As in the two-fold upper bound proof (Theorem
1
), a mean-value expansion gives

\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})=-\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))^{-1}\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})),

(166)

for some
\tilde{\theta}(x_{0})
between
\hat{\theta}(x_{0})
and
\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})
, where

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}
is the two-fold estimating equation in Theorem
1
.
Since
\eta_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=0
, the two-fold decomposition yields

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))=I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})).

(167)

Step 2: Jacobian stability.

Under the rate conditions (
14
), the split Jacobian estimator is stable on
\Theta_{0}
and invertible with probability tending to one. In particular,

\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))^{-1}\to_{p}J_{\lambda}(x_{0})^{-1},\qquad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)^{-1}\big|=O_{p}(1).

(168)

Combining (
166
) and (
167
),

\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})=-\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))^{-1}I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+R_{n,N},

(169)

where

R_{n,N}:=-\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))^{-1}\Big(I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\Big).

Thus, to prove (
165
), it suffices to show

\frac{I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}\to N(0,1),

(170)

and

\frac{I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=o_{p}(1),\qquad\frac{I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=o_{p}(1).

(171)

Step 3: Lindeberg–Feller CLT for
I_{1}/\sqrt{V(x_{0})}
.

Since
I_{1}
remains unaffected by the two-fold splitting procedure, (
170
) has already been established in the proof of Theorem
5
in Appendix
D.3
.

Step 4:
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
and
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
are negligible.

By Lemma
10
,
\sqrt{V(x_{0})}\gtrsim\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)/n}
.
By Lemma
12
(i),

|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|=O_{p}\left(\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}\right),

hence

\frac{|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=O_{p}\left(\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log N}{N}}\right)\to 0

by (
14
).
By Lemma
12
(ii),

|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|=O_{p}\left(B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}+\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\right),

and therefore

\frac{|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))|}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=O_{p}\left(B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)}\left(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}\log N}{N}\right)\right)\to 0

by (
14
). This proves (
171
).

Step 5: conclude.

Multiply (
169
) by
J_{\lambda}(x_{0})/\sqrt{V(x_{0})}
:

\displaystyle\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big)}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})}{\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))}\frac{I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})}{\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))}\frac{I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}.

By (
168
),

\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})}{\hat{J}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\tilde{\theta}(x_{0}))}\to_{p}1
.
By (
170
),

\frac{I_{1}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}\to N(0,1)
.
By (
171
),

\frac{I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))+I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0}))}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=o_{p}(1)
.
Slutsky’s theorem yields (
165
).

Step 6: bias is negligible.
Following the same arguments as in Step 6 of the proof of Theorem
5
in Appendix
D.3
, we obtain

\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}(x_{0})\big)}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}=\frac{J_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big(\hat{\theta}(x_{0})-\theta_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big)}{\sqrt{V(x_{0})}}+o(1),

and combining with (
165
) completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1
.
We also provide the following proof.

Proof of Corollary
1
.

The proof of Corollary
1
follows identical arguments to the proof of Corollary
2
in Appendix
D.3
, and is therefore omitted.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2
.
In the following, we prove the minimax result (Theorem
2
) in Section
4
.
We first recall the following useful lemma from
Tsybakov 2009
, and then give the proof of Theorem
2
.

Lemma 14

(Section 2.2 in
Tsybakov 2009
)
.

Let
P_{0},P_{1}
be two distributions and let
\theta(P)
be a real-valued parameter.
Write
\Delta:=|\theta(P_{1})-\theta(P_{0})|
.
Then for any estimator
\tilde{\theta}
,

\max\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta(P_{0}))^{2}\big],\ \mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}\big[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta(P_{1}))^{2}\big]\Big\}\ \geq\ \frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}\Big(1-\mathrm{TV}(P_{0},P_{1})\Big).

Moreover, by Pinsker’s inequality,
\mathrm{TV}(P_{0},P_{1})\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{KL}(P_{0}\|P_{1})/2}
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

We emphasize that the minimax lower bound will be proved by restricting
\mathcal{P}

to a simpler conditional mean Gaussian submodel.
Throughout this proof, the minimax lower bound is proved by restricting to
the conditional-mean submodel

\ell(y;\theta)=y-\theta,\qquad\theta_{0}(x_{0})=\mathbb{E}[Y|X=x_{0}]=:m(x_{0}),

which is contained in the full model class
\mathcal{P}
. For conditional mean Gaussian submodel
\mathcal{P}
,
(i)
X\sim\rho_{X}
for some
\rho_{X}
on
\mathcal{X}
;
(ii) the conditional mean function
m(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y|X=x]
belongs to
\mathcal{H}
and

\|m\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}
;
(iii) the conditional noise satisfies
\mathrm{Var}(Y|X)\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}
a.s.
In the lower bound proofs we will further restrict to the Gaussian submodel

Y=m(X)+\varepsilon
with
\varepsilon|X\sim N(0,\underline{\sigma}^{2})
;
this restriction is valid since it only shrinks
\mathcal{P}
.

Step 1: reduce to two-point lower bounds.

For any two distributions
P_{0},P_{1}\in\mathcal{P}
we have

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\ \geq\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\max\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}^{(0)})^{2}],\ \mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}^{(1)})^{2}]\Big\},

where
\theta_{0}^{(i)}:=\theta_{0}^{(i)}(x_{0})
denotes the pointwise target under
P_{i}
.

Step 2: stochastic two-point bound.

Fix an arbitrary
P\in\mathcal{P}
and keep its
\rho_{X}
and
K
fixed.
Define the normalized function

h_{\lambda}:=\frac{w_{x_{0},\lambda}}{\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}}\in\mathcal{H}.

(172)

For the bilinear identity, note that

(K_{X}\otimes K_{X})g=\langle g,K_{X}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,K_{X}=g(X)K_{X}
and hence for any
h,g\in\mathcal{H}
,
\langle h,(K_{X}\otimes K_{X})g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle h,K_{X}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\langle g,K_{X}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=h(X)g(X)
.
Taking expectation yields

\langle h,T_{K}g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathbb{E}[h(X)g(X)].

(173)

Use
(T_{K}+\lambda I)w_{x_{0},\lambda}=K_{x_{0}}

and take the
\mathcal{H}
inner product with
w_{x_{0},\lambda}
:

\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},T_{K}w_{x_{0},\lambda}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=D(x_{0};\lambda),

and apply (
173
) with
h=g=w_{x_{0},\lambda}
to identify

\langle w_{x_{0},\lambda},T_{K}w_{x_{0},\lambda}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
. Then we have

D(x_{0};\lambda)=\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}\big]+\lambda\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}.

(174)

Then
h_{\lambda}(x_{0})=\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}
and by (
174
),

\mathbb{E}[h_{\lambda}(X)^{2}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]}{D(x_{0};\lambda)}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]}{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\leq 1.

We now choose the signal amplitude
\delta=\delta(\lambda)
explicitly so that:
(a) the KL divergence is small and hence TV is bounded away from 1, and
(b) the RKHS constraint
\|m\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}
is satisfied.
Set

\delta(\lambda):=\min\Big\{\frac{\underline{\sigma}}{\sqrt{32n}},\ \frac{B_{\eta}}{2\|h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}}\Big\}.

(175)

This ensures
\delta(\lambda)\leq\underline{\sigma}/\sqrt{32n}
and also

\|\delta(\lambda)h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}/2
.

Consider the Gaussian regression alternatives

Y=\pm\delta(\lambda)\,h_{\lambda}(X)+\varepsilon,\qquad\varepsilon|X\sim N(0,\underline{\sigma}^{2}),

and let
P_{+}
and
P_{-}
denote the induced joint laws of
(X,Y)
.
Both have the same marginal
\rho_{X}
, and both satisfy
\|m_{\pm}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}
by construction,
so
P_{\pm}\in\mathcal{P}
.

The unlabeled sample has the same law under
P_{+}
and
P_{-}
, hence

\mathrm{KL}(P_{+}^{(n,N)}\|P_{-}^{(n,N)})=\mathrm{KL}(P_{+}^{(n)}\|P_{-}^{(n)})
.

The pointwise targets satisfy

\theta_{0}^{(+)}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}^{(-)}(x_{0})=\big(+\delta(\lambda)h_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big)-\big(-\delta(\lambda)h_{\lambda}(x_{0})\big)=2\delta(\lambda)\sqrt{D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

Thus, in Lemma
14
,
\Delta^{2}/4=\delta(\lambda)^{2}D(x_{0};\lambda)
.

For Gaussian regression with common variance
\underline{\sigma}^{2}
,

\mathrm{KL}(P_{+}^{(n)}\|P_{-}^{(n)})=\frac{n}{2\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big((+\delta h_{\lambda}(X))-(-\delta h_{\lambda}(X))\big)^{2}\Big]=\frac{2n\delta(\lambda)^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[h_{\lambda}(X)^{2}]\leq\frac{2n\delta(\lambda)^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}.

Since
\delta(\lambda)\leq\underline{\sigma}/\sqrt{32n}
by (
175
),
we have
\mathrm{KL}\leq 1/16
, hence
\mathrm{TV}(P_{+}^{(n,N)},P_{-}^{(n,N)})\leq 1/4
by Pinsker.

Applying Lemma
14
yields

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\max\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{+}}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}^{(+)})^{2}],\mathbb{E}_{P_{-}}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}^{(-)})^{2}]\}\ \geq\ \delta(\lambda)^{2}D(x_{0};\lambda)\Big(1-\mathrm{TV}\Big)\ \geq\ \frac{3}{4}\,\delta(\lambda)^{2}D(x_{0};\lambda).

Because
P
was arbitrary and the construction keeps
(K,\rho_{X})
fixed,
this implies that uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\ \gtrsim\ \delta(\lambda)^{2}\,D(x_{0};\lambda).

(176)

Finally, note that
D(x_{0};\lambda)\geq\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
and also
\frac{1}{n}\geq\frac{1}{2}v_{n,N}
.
In the regime where the KL constraint is active (i.e.
\delta(\lambda)=\underline{\sigma}/\sqrt{32n}
),
(
176
) gives
\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\gtrsim v_{n,N}\,\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
.
In the complementary regime where the RKHS constraint is active,
(
176
) yields a bound of order
B_{\eta}^{2}\lambda D(x_{0};\lambda)

(see the calculation in Step 3 below), which is compatible with the bias scale.

Step 3: approximation two-point bound.

Keep the same
\rho_{X}
and
K
and again write
w_{x_{0},\lambda}
and
D(x_{0};\lambda)
,
and define
h_{\lambda}
as in (
172
).
Consider the Gaussian regression alternatives

Y=\pm B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda}\,h_{\lambda}(X)+\varepsilon,\qquad\varepsilon|X\sim N(0,\underline{\sigma}^{2}).

We verify
\|B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda}\,h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}
:

\|B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda}\,h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=B_{\eta}^{2}\lambda\frac{\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{D(x_{0};\lambda)}\leq B_{\eta}^{2}\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda}=B_{\eta}^{2},

where we used
\lambda\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\leq D(x_{0};\lambda)
from the energy identity (
174
).
Hence both alternatives lie in
\{m:\|m\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq B_{\eta}\}
and thus belong to
\mathcal{P}
.

The pointwise separation is

\theta_{0}^{(+)}(x_{0})-\theta_{0}^{(-)}(x_{0})=2B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda}\,h_{\lambda}(x_{0})=2B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda D(x_{0};\lambda)},

so
\Delta^{2}/4=B_{\eta}^{2}\,\lambda\,D(x_{0};\lambda)
.

Using again
\mathbb{E}[h_{\lambda}(X)^{2}]\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(P_{+}^{(n,N)}\|P_{-}^{(n,N)})=\mathrm{KL}(P_{+}^{(n)}\|P_{-}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(2B_{\eta}\sqrt{\lambda}\,h_{\lambda}(X)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{2nB_{\eta}^{2}\lambda}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[h_{\lambda}(X)^{2}]\leq\frac{2nB_{\eta}^{2}\lambda}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}.

Whenever
\lambda\leq\underline{\sigma}^{2}/(32nB_{\eta}^{2})
, we have
\mathrm{KL}\leq 1/16
and thus

\mathrm{TV}\leq 1/4
.

Applying Lemma
14
yields

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\ \gtrsim\ B_{\eta}^{2}\,\lambda\,D(x_{0};\lambda)\qquad\text{for all }\lambda\leq\underline{\sigma}^{2}/(32nB_{\eta}^{2}).

(177)

Step 4: conclude.

From Step 2 we have a valid two-point lower bound for this
\lambda
of the form

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\gtrsim\delta(\lambda)^{2}D(x_{0};\lambda)
, with
\delta(\lambda)
defined in
(
175
). In particular, in the KL-active regime

\delta(\lambda)=\underline{\sigma}/\sqrt{32n}
we obtain

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\gtrsim v_{n,N}\,\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
using
D(x_{0};\lambda)\geq\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
and
1/n\gtrsim v_{n,N}
,
and in the RKHS-active regime the same bound is at least of order
B_{\eta}^{2}\lambda D(x_{0};\lambda)

because
\|h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}/D(x_{0};\lambda)\leq 1/\lambda
implies

\delta(\lambda)=\frac{B_{\eta}}{2\|h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}}\ \to\ \delta(\lambda)^{2}D(x_{0};\lambda)=\frac{B_{\eta}^{2}}{4}\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)}{\|h_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}=\frac{B_{\eta}^{2}}{4}\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)^{2}}{\|w_{x_{0},\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\geq\frac{B_{\eta}^{2}}{4}\lambda D(x_{0};\lambda).

Moreover, Step 3 supplies the explicit bias-scale bound (
177
)
whenever
\lambda\leq\underline{\sigma}^{2}/(32nB_{\eta}^{2})
.

Putting these together (and absorbing constants), we obtain that for each
\lambda

\inf_{\tilde{\theta}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[(\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{0}(x_{0}))^{2}]\ \gtrsim\ \Big\{v_{n,N}\,\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]\Big\}\ \vee\ \Big\{B_{\eta}^{2}\,\lambda\,D(x_{0};\lambda)\Big\},

up to constants depending only on
(\kappa,B_{\eta},\underline{\sigma}^{2})
.
Since the inequality holds for
\lambda\leq\underline{\sigma}^{2}/(32nB_{\eta}^{2})
., taking
\inf_{\lambda>0}

on the right-hand side yields (
13
). Finally, because
P\in\mathcal{P}

was arbitrary in Steps 2–3 (with the constructions staying inside
\mathcal{P}
),
we may insert
\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}
in front of
\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)^{2}]
and
D(x_{0};\lambda)
without affecting validity,
obtaining the stated form in (
13
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.
We prove the optimal sampling result in Section
5
.

Proof of Proposition
2
.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle V(x_{0})\cdot C

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}}{n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}_{f}}{N}\right)(c_{l}n+c_{u}N)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\sigma_{Y-f}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{c_{l}n}+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{c_{u}N}\right)^{2}=\left(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}_{Y-f}\,c_{l}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{2}_{f}\,c_{u}}\right)^{2}.

Dividing both sides by
C
yields the minimum variance lower bound
V_{\min}(C)
. The equality condition for Cauchy-Schwarz holds if and only if

\frac{\sigma_{Y-f}/\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{c_{l}n}}=\frac{\sigma_{f}/\sqrt{N}}{\sqrt{c_{u}N}}\quad\implies\quad\frac{n}{N}=\frac{\sigma_{Y-f}/\sqrt{c_{l}}}{\sigma_{f}/\sqrt{c_{u}}}.

Substituting this back into the active budget constraint
c_{l}n+c_{u}N=C
yields the optimal sample allocations
n^{\star}
and
N^{\star}
, completing the proof.
∎

Proof of (
15
) in Section
6
.
We provide the proof of (
15
) in Section
6
here.

Proof of (
15
).

Conditionally on
X=x
, let
D=\ell(Y;\theta)-\ell(f(x);\theta)
. Because
\ell(f(x);\theta)
is deterministic given
X=x
, the conditional variance of
Z(\theta)
is

\mathrm{Var}\big(Z(\theta)|X=x\big)=\mathrm{Var}\left(\ell(f(x);\theta)+\frac{O}{\pi}D|X=x\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\mathrm{Var}(OD|X=x).

For
\mathrm{Var}(OD|X=x)
, we have
\mathrm{Var}(OD|X=x)=\mathbb{E}[O^{2}D^{2}|X=x]-\big(\mathbb{E}[OD|X=x]\big)^{2}
. Since the observation indicator
O\in\{0,1\}
is a Bernoulli random variable, we have
O^{2}=O
. Furthermore,
O
is independent of
(X,Y)
with
\mathbb{E}[O]=\pi
. Therefore, the conditional moments are:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[O^{2}D^{2}|X=x]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[O]\mathbb{E}[D^{2}|X=x]=\pi\mathbb{E}[D^{2}|X=x],

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}[OD|X=x]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{E}[O]\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}=\pi^{2}\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}.

Substituting these into the variance formula, and applying the identity
\mathbb{E}[D^{2}|X=x]=\mathrm{Var}(D|X=x)+\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(OD|X=x)

\displaystyle=\pi\mathbb{E}[D^{2}|X=x]-\pi^{2}\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\Big(\mathrm{Var}(D|X=x)+\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}\Big)-\pi^{2}\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\mathrm{Var}(D|X=x)+\pi(1-\pi)\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}.

Noting that
\mathrm{Var}(D|X=x)=\mathrm{Var}\big(\ell(Y;\theta)|X=x\big)
and
\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]=\eta(x;\theta)-\ell(f(x);\theta)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(Z(\theta)|X=x\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\Big[\pi\mathrm{Var}(D|X=x)+\pi(1-\pi)\big(\mathbb{E}[D|X=x]\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{Var}\big(\ell(Y;\theta)|X=x\big)+\frac{1-\pi}{\pi}\big(\eta(x;\theta)-\ell(f(x);\theta)\big)^{2},

which is exactly (
15
).
∎

E.2
Proofs of Technical Lemmas in Appendix
E.1

Proof of Lemma
12
.

Step 1.

From the definition of
\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}
and
\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}
,

\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)-\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta\overline{w}(X_{i})r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\Delta w^{(3-m)}(\tilde{X}_{u})u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta).

Also
\mu(\theta)=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y;\theta)K_{X}]=\mathbb{E}[r(Y,X;\theta)K_{X}]+\mathbb{E}[u(X;\theta)K_{X}]
, hence

I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)=\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)r(Y,X;\theta)]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)u(X;\theta)].

Because
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=|\mathcal{I}_{2}|=N/2
and
\Delta\overline{w}=\frac{1}{2}(\Delta w^{(1)}+\Delta w^{(2)})
,

\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)u(X;\theta)]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\Delta w^{(m)}(X)u(X;\theta)].

On the other hand, for each
m
, conditional on the training fold
\{\tilde{X}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{3-m}\}
,
the function
\Delta w^{(3-m)}
is measurable and
\{\tilde{X}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\}
are i.i.d. and independent of that training fold, so

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2}{N}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\Delta w^{(3-m)}(\tilde{X}_{u})u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)\Big|\{\tilde{X}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{3-m}\}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\Delta w^{(3-m)}(X)u(X;\theta)].

Averaging over
m=1,2
shows that subtracting
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
from
\hat{\eta}_{\lambda}-\tilde{\eta}_{\lambda}

indeed removes the (conditional) mean, leaving a sum of centered empirical fluctuations. This is exactly
I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}
by definition.

Step 2:
\|\Delta w^{(m)}\|_{\infty}
bound on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
.

Fix
m\in\{1,2\}
and work on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
.
From the whitening identity,

(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I)^{-1}=(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big(I+A^{(m)}(\lambda)\big)^{-1}(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1/2},

so on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
,
\|(I+A^{(m)}(\lambda))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2
implies the operator inequality

(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I)^{-1}\preceq 2(T_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}
.
Therefore, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle|\hat{w}^{(m)}_{x_{0},\lambda}(x)|

\displaystyle=|\langle K_{x},(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\langle K_{x},(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}\sqrt{\langle K_{x_{0}},(\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}+\lambda I)^{-1}K_{x_{0}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}\leq 2\sqrt{D(x;\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

Also
|w_{x_{0},\lambda}(x)|\leq\sqrt{D(x;\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}
.
Hence on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
,

\|\Delta w^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\leq 3\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\sqrt{D(x;\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\leq 3B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)},

where we used Assumption
4
to get
\sup_{x}D(x;\lambda)\leq B_{\phi}^{2}D(\lambda)
.
On
\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{E}_{1}\cap\mathscr{E}_{2}
this implies

\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}(\|\Delta w^{(1)}\|_{\infty}+\|\Delta w^{(2)}\|_{\infty})\leq 3B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}.

Step 3:
L^{2}
bounds for
\Delta w^{(m)}
and
\Delta\overline{w}
.

Apply Lemma
5
fold-wise with
N^{\prime}=|\mathcal{I}_{m}|=N/2
and
\hat{T}_{K}=\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}
.
Then on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
, with probability at least
1-(2|\mathcal{I}_{m}|)^{-1}=1-\frac{1}{N}
,

\|\Delta w^{(m)}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\leq c_{0}B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}}\leq c_{0}B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}.

By the triangle inequality,

\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\big(\|\Delta w^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta w^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq c_{0}B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}

on the intersection of the two fold-wise
L^{2}
events. A union bound makes their total failure probability
\leq 2/N
.

Step 4: bound
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Since
I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\langle\Delta w^{(m)},\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
,
|I_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{2}|\langle\Delta w^{(m)},\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|
.
For each fixed
m
, Lemma
8
applies verbatim to the fold operator
\hat{T}_{K}^{(m)}

with sample size
N^{\prime}=|\mathcal{I}_{m}|=N/2
and stability event
\mathscr{E}_{m}
.
Thus (using
\log(N/2)\leq\log N
) we obtain, for all sufficiently large
N
,

|\langle\Delta w^{(m)},\mu(\theta)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq 8\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log(N/2)}{N/2}}\leq 8\sqrt{2}\kappa\|\eta(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}

with conditional failure probability at most
2(N^{\prime})^{-1}=4/N
on
\mathscr{E}_{m}
.
Unconditionally, for each
m
,
\mathbb{P}(\text{the above bound fails})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{m}^{c})+\frac{4}{N}
. By Lemma
11
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{m}^{c})\leq\frac{2}{N}
.
Therefore the failure probability for each fold is at most
\frac{6}{N}
, and by a union bound over
m=1,2
,
the bound for both folds holds with probability at least
1-\frac{12}{N}
.
Averaging over the two folds gives (i).

Step 5: labeled fluctuation bound for
I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Define the labeled centered fluctuation

I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta\overline{w}(X_{i})r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)r(Y,X;\theta)].

Condition on all unlabeled covariates (so
\Delta\overline{w}
is fixed).
Then the summands are i.i.d. mean-zero, bounded by

|\Delta\overline{w}(X_{i})r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)|\leq B_{Y-f}(\theta)\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{\infty}
,
and their conditional variance is at most
B_{Y-f}^{2}(\theta)\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{L^{2}(\rho_{X})}^{2}
.
Bernstein’s inequality in the form

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{2\sigma^{2}t}{n}}+\frac{2Mt}{3n}\Big|\Delta\overline{w}\right)\leq 2e^{-t}

with
\sigma^{2}=B_{Y-f}^{2}\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
and
M=B_{Y-f}\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{\infty}

yields: for any
t\geq 1
, with conditional probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

|I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}|\leq B_{Y-f}(\theta)\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{L^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{n}}+\frac{2}{3}B_{Y-f}(\theta)\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{\infty}\frac{t}{n}.

Take
t:=2\log n
so
2e^{-t}\leq 2n^{-2}
.
On
\mathscr{E}
and the
L^{2}
events from Step 3, and using Step 2 for
\|\Delta\overline{w}\|_{\infty}
, we get

|I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}|\leq B_{Y-f}(\theta)c_{0}B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{4\log n}{n}}+\frac{2}{3}B_{Y-f}(\theta)3B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\frac{2\log n}{n}.

Using
\sqrt{\frac{2\log N}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{4\log n}{n}}=\sqrt{8\frac{\log n\log N}{nN}}
, which we bound via
\sqrt{ab}\leq\frac{a+b}{2}
with
a=\frac{4\log n}{n}
and
b=\frac{2\log N}{N}
:

\sqrt{\frac{8\log n\log N}{nN}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{4\log n}{n}+\frac{2\log N}{N}\right)=\frac{2\log n}{n}+\frac{\log N}{N}.

Thus, on the same event and with failure probability at most
2n^{-2}
,

\displaystyle|I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}|

\displaystyle\leq B_{Y-f}(\theta)B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left[c_{0}\left(\frac{2\log n}{n}+\frac{\log N}{N}\right)+4\frac{\log n}{n}\right]

\displaystyle=B_{Y-f}(\theta)B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left[(2c_{0}+4)\frac{\log n}{n}+c_{0}\frac{\log N}{N}\right].

Step 6: unlabeled fluctuation bound for
I_{2,U}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Define

I_{2,U}^{\mathrm{split}}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{2}\left\{\frac{2}{N}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\Delta w^{(3-m)}(\tilde{X}_{u})u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[\Delta w^{(3-m)}(X)u(X;\theta)]\right\}.

Fix
m
and condition on the training fold
\{\tilde{X}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{3-m}\}
.
Then
\Delta w^{(3-m)}
is fixed and
\{\tilde{X}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\}
are i.i.d.
As above,
|u(\cdot;\theta)|\leq B_{f}(\theta)
.
Bernstein with
|\mathcal{I}_{m}|=N/2
gives: for any
t\geq 1
, with conditional probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle|\Delta_{m}|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{N}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\Delta w^{(3-m)}(\tilde{X}_{u})u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[\Delta w^{(3-m)}(X)u(X;\theta)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq B_{f}(\theta)\|\Delta w^{(3-m)}\|_{L^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}}+\frac{2}{3}B_{f}(\theta)\|\Delta w^{(3-m)}\|_{\infty}\frac{t}{|\mathcal{I}_{m}|}.

Take
t:=2\log N
so
2e^{-t}\leq 2N^{-2}
.
On
\mathscr{E}
and the fold-wise
L^{2}
events from Step 3, and using Step 2 for
\|\Delta w^{(3-m)}\|_{\infty}
, we obtain

\left|\Delta_{m}\right|\leq B_{f}(\theta)c_{0}B_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)\log N}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{4\log N}{N/2}}+\frac{2}{3}B_{f}(\theta)3B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\frac{2\log N}{N/2}.

Since
\sqrt{\frac{4\log N}{N/2}}=\sqrt{\frac{8\log N}{N}}
and
\frac{2\log N}{N/2}=\frac{4\log N}{N}
, the variance term becomes
\sqrt{\frac{2\log N}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{8\log N}{N}}=\sqrt{16}\frac{\log N}{N}=4\frac{\log N}{N}
.
This yields

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4c_{0}B_{f}(\theta)B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\frac{\log N}{N}+8B_{f}(\theta)B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\frac{\log N}{N}

\displaystyle=(4c_{0}+8)B_{f}(\theta)B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\frac{\log N}{N}.

A union bound over
m=1,2
makes the total failure probability for these two Bernstein events at most
4N^{-2}
.
Finally, since
|I_{2,U}^{\mathrm{split}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{2}|\Delta_{m}|
, the same bound holds for
|I_{2,U}^{\mathrm{split}}|
.

Step 7: conclude.

On the intersection of:
(i)
\mathscr{E}
(failure probability
\leq 4/N
since
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}_{m}^{c})\leq 2/N
by Lemma
11
),
(ii) the two fold-wise
L^{2}
events in Step 3 (failure probability
\leq 2/N
),
(iii) the labeled Bernstein event in Step 5 (failure probability
\leq 2n^{-2}
),
(iv) the two unlabeled Bernstein events in Step 6 (failure probability
\leq 4N^{-2}
),
we have

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}|

\displaystyle\leq|I_{2,R}^{\mathrm{split}}|+|I_{2,U}^{\mathrm{split}}|

\displaystyle\leq B_{\phi}\sqrt{D(\lambda)D(x_{0};\lambda)}\left[(2c_{0}+4)B_{Y-f}(\theta)\frac{\log n}{n}+\Big(c_{0}B_{Y-f}(\theta)+(4c_{0}+8)B_{f}(\theta)\Big)\frac{\log N}{N}\right].

The claimed success probability follows by the union bound:

1-\left(\frac{4}{N}+\frac{2}{N}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}+\frac{4}{N^{2}}\right)=1-\frac{6}{N}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}-\frac{4}{N^{2}}
.
This completes the proof of (ii), and (i) was proved in Step 4.
∎

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Step 1: decomposition into
J_{1}^{\mathrm{split}}+J_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}+J_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}
.

Define

\displaystyle J_{\lambda}(x_{0};\theta)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)\big],

\displaystyle J_{1}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X_{i})\partial_{\theta}r(Y_{i},X_{i};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)]\Big\}

\displaystyle\hphantom{:=}+\Big\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u=1}^{N}w_{x_{0},\lambda}(\tilde{X}_{u})\partial_{\theta}u(\tilde{X}_{u};\theta)-\mathbb{E}[w_{x_{0},\lambda}(X)\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)]\Big\},

\displaystyle J_{3}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)\partial_{\theta}r(Y,X;\theta)]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta\overline{w}(X)\partial_{\theta}u(X;\theta)],

\displaystyle J_{2}^{\mathrm{split}}(x_{0};\theta)
